- Brief: NAG-02: Rate-limit enforcement under burst traffic — requests beyond the per-connection window are rejected with RATE_LIMITED.
- Parameters:
  - `<unnamed>` (NetworkHardeningPhase2AdversarialGuard): n/a
  - `<unnamed>` (NAG02_BurstTrafficRateLimited): n/a

#### `TEST(NetworkHardeningPhase2AdversarialGuard, NAG03_ClientBannedAfterThresholdViolations)`
- Source: `tests/network/test_network_hardening_phase2_focused.cpp`:409
- Brief: NAG-03: CLIENT_BANNED after sustained rate-limit threshold violations.
- Parameters:
  - `<unnamed>` (NetworkHardeningPhase2AdversarialGuard): n/a
  - `<unnamed>` (NAG03_ClientBannedAfterThresholdViolations): n/a
- Details: After kRateLimitBanThreshold consecutive violations, new requests must receive CLIENT_BANNED rather than RATE_LIMITED.

#### `TEST(NetworkHardeningPhase2AdversarialGuard, NAG04_AuthTimeoutIsConnectionClosing)`
- Source: `tests/network/test_network_hardening_phase2_focused.cpp`:431
- Brief: NAG-04: Stalled authentication handshake → AUTH_TIMEOUT, which is connection-closing (prevents resource exhaustion from hung sessions).
- Parameters:
  - `<unnamed>` (NetworkHardeningPhase2AdversarialGuard): n/a
  - `<unnamed>` (NAG04_AuthTimeoutIsConnectionClosing): n/a

#### `TEST(NetworkHardeningPhase2AdversarialGuard, NAG05_RevocationPriorityOverExpiry)`
- Source: `tests/network/test_network_hardening_phase2_focused.cpp`:450
- Brief: NAG-05: SESSION_REVOKED takes priority over SESSION_EXPIRED.
- Parameters:
  - `<unnamed>` (NetworkHardeningPhase2AdversarialGuard): n/a
  - `<unnamed>` (NAG05_RevocationPriorityOverExpiry): n/a
- Details: A revoked session must always be rejected with SESSION_REVOKED regardless of whether it is also expired, preventing expiry-bypass attacks.

#### `TEST(NetworkHardeningPhase2AdversarialGuard, NAG06_UnavailableRateLimiterFailClosed)`
- Source: `tests/network/test_network_hardening_phase2_focused.cpp`:468
- Brief: NAG-06: Rate-limiter unavailability → fail-closed deny (not fail-open).
- Parameters:
  - `<unnamed>` (NetworkHardeningPhase2AdversarialGuard): n/a
  - `<unnamed>` (NAG06_UnavailableRateLimiterFailClosed): n/a
- Details: When the rate-limiting backend is unreachable, no request must be let through — fail-open would allow adversarial traffic to bypass limits.

#### `TEST(NetworkHardeningPhase2AdversarialGuard, NAG07_BackpressureExceededTransientNotClosing)`
- Source: `tests/network/test_network_hardening_phase2_focused.cpp`:484
- Brief: NAG-07: BACKPRESSURE_EXCEEDED is transient, NOT connection-closing.
- Parameters:
  - `<unnamed>` (NetworkHardeningPhase2AdversarialGuard): n/a
  - `<unnamed>` (NAG07_BackpressureExceededTransientNotClosing): n/a
- Details: Backpressure is a temporary condition; the connection must remain alive so that the client can retry after the queue drains.

#### `TEST(NetworkHardeningPhase2AdversarialGuard, NAG08_ClusterErrorsNotConnectionClosing)`

  - `<unnamed>` (NetworkHardeningPhase2AdversarialGuard): n/a
  - `<unnamed>` (NAG08_ClusterErrorsNotConnectionClosing): n/a

#### `TEST(NetworkQuicProduction, NQP02_FallbackDelayContractSanity)`
- Source: `tests/network/test_network_lifecycle_guardrails_focused.cpp`:405
- Brief: NQP-02: kTransportFallbackDelay is a positive duration bounded below the auth handshake timeout — the fallback window is a real, sane interval.
- Parameters:
  - `<unnamed>` (NetworkQuicProduction): n/a
  - `<unnamed>` (NQP02_FallbackDelayContractSanity): n/a
- Details: Note: kTransportFallbackDelay governs when a transport path is considered failed and a fallback path is activated. It is intentionally larger than a 0-RTT window (QUIC 0-RTT resumption completes at the TLS layer before fallback detection even triggers). This test validates the constant's structural invariants, not the 0-RTT handshake duration itself.

- Source: `tests/network/test_network_transport_resilience_focused.cpp`:345

  - `<unnamed>` (NTR06_GrpcFallbackExhaustionExplicitError): n/a

#### `TEST(NetworkTransportResilience, NTR07_PoolDrainingRejectsAndBounds)`
- Source: `tests/network/test_network_transport_resilience_focused.cpp`:367

  - `<unnamed>` (NTR07_PoolDrainingRejectsAndBounds): n/a

- Source: `tests/network/test_network_transport_resilience_focused.cpp`:400

### themis::bench::nrg_p

#### `void BM_NRGP1_WsFrameParseThroughput(benchmark::State &state)`
- Source: `benchmarks/network/bench_network_protocol_path.cpp`:282
- Brief: NRG-P1: WS frame parse throughput.
- Parameters:
  - `state` (benchmark::State &): n/a
- Details: Gate: ≥ 5 M frames/sec. Throughput is measured implicitly: Google Benchmark reports items/sec when SetItemsProcessed() is called. The benchmark is configured with ->Iterations(5'000'000) to accumulate enough samples for a stable reading. Stub basis: parseWsFrame() replicates the real WS frame parser decision tree (FIN/RSV/opcode/mask/len validation) without I/O. Hardware baseline pending: Representative hardware baselines must be collected before Wave D sign-off (see ROADMAP.md mid-term items [~]).

#### `void BM_NRGP2_TcpZeroCopyOverhead(benchmark::State &state)`
- Source: `benchmarks/network/bench_network_protocol_path.cpp`:324
- Brief: NRG-P2: Zero-copy frame builder overhead for a 64 KiB payload.
- Parameters:
  - `state` (benchmark::State &): n/a
- Details: Gate: ≤ 5 µs per call. Stub basis: StubZeroCopyBuilder::writeToWithSendfile() models the bookkeeping cost of the real ZeroCopyFrameBuilder::writeToWithSendfile() path (sendfile params + atomic counter update) without syscall overhead. The real sendfile(2) dominates at the OS layer; this gate covers the userspace overhead around it. Hardware baseline pending: Representative hardware baselines must be collected before Wave D sign-off.

#### `void BM_NRGP3_Quic0RttHandshake(benchmark::State &state)`
- Source: `benchmarks/network/bench_network_protocol_path.cpp`:364
- Brief: NRG-P3: QUIC 0-RTT resumption path overhead.
- Parameters:
  - `state` (benchmark::State &): n/a
- Details: Gate: ≤ 50 ms per handshake. Stub basis: Models session ticket lookup + 2-round key schedule step + anti-replay check. The real 0-RTT path adds TLS AEAD decryption overhead and a UDP round-trip; this stub isolates the in-process cost. Hardware baseline pending: Real QUIC 0-RTT measurements require the THEMIS_ENABLE_HTTP3 build and a loopback network. This stub establishes the gate before hardware numbers are collected.

#### `void BM_NRGP4_GrpcMetadataOverhead(benchmark::State &state)`
- Source: `benchmarks/network/bench_network_protocol_path.cpp`:413
- Brief: NRG-P4: gRPC metadata processing overhead per call.
- Parameters:
  - `state` (benchmark::State &): n/a
- Details: Gate: ≤ 10 µs per call. Stub basis: Models HPACK-style header construction cost + method-path routing table lookup + trailer encoding. The real gRPC path additionally performs HTTP/2 frame assembly and HPACK huffman coding; this stub covers the in-process routing and metadata cost. Hardware baseline pending: Representative hardware baselines must be collected before Wave D sign-off.

#### `bool retryWithPolicy(const WireRetryPolicy &policy, std::function< bool()> op, std::function< void(uint32_t, int64_t)> on_fail={}) noexcept`
- Source: `src/network/wire_retry_policy.cpp`:105
- Brief: Execute op with retry governed by policy.
- Parameters:
  - `policy` (const WireRetryPolicy &): Retry parameters.
  - `op` (std::function< bool()>): Operation to retry; returns true on success.
  - `on_fail` (std::function< void(uint32_t, int64_t)>): Optional callback invoked on each failure with (attempt, delay_ms).
- Return: true if op succeeded within the allowed attempts; false otherwise.
- Details: op must be callable as bool(), returning true on success and false on a retryable failure. Exceptions thrown by op are caught and treated as transient failures; they do not propagate through the retry loop. policy Retry parameters. op Operation to retry; returns true on success. on_fail Optional callback invoked on each failure with (attempt, delay_ms). true if op succeeded within the allowed attempts; false otherwise.

#### `void recordFailure()`
- Source: `include/network/adaptive_circuit_breaker.h`:212
- Brief: Notify that the last request failed.
- Parameters: none
- Details: Record Failure. In CLOSED: increments failure counter; opens circuit when failure_threshold is reached. Adaptive mode may lower the effective threshold on repeated trips. In HALF_OPEN: immediately re-opens the circuit. Calls: load(), lock(), adaptThresholdOnTrip(), transitionTo().

#### `void recordFailure(const ErrorClass &error_class)`
- Source: `include/network/adaptive_circuit_breaker.h`:228
- Brief: Notify that the last request failed with a specific error class.
- Parameters:
  - `error_class` (const ErrorClass &): Input parameter.
- Details: Record Failure. If the error class has a configured override in Config::error_class_configs, its consecutive-failure counter is incremented separately. When that counter reaches the class threshold the circuit trips immediately, regardless of the global counter. Falls through to the global recordFailure() accounting as well. error_class String label for the error category (e.g., "timeout", "connection", "auth"). Unknown classes are tracked globally only (no per-class threshold applied). error_class Input parameter. Calls: load(), lock(), recordErrorClassFailure(), adaptThresholdOnTrip(), transitionTo().

- Details: Record. If the ring buffer is at capacity the oldest entry is silently dropped (stats.total_evicted is incremented). event The event to record. If the event's timestamp is the default-constructed (epoch), it is replaced with the current wall-clock time. event Input parameter. Calls: std::chrono::system_clock::now(), void(), lk(), size(), pop_front(), push_back(), updateCounters(), cb_copy().

  - `token` (const std::string &): Input parameter.
- Details: Record Auth. The raw token is never stored. A truncated SHA-256 hash (16 lower-case hex chars) is derived and stored in the event's token_hash field. success true → AUTH_SUCCESS; false → AUTH_FAILURE. remote_address Peer IP[:port] string. connection_id Server-assigned connection identifier. principal User / principal name (may be empty). token Raw token (hashed; never persisted as-is). success Input parameter. remote_address Input parameter. connection_id Identifier of the connection. principal Input parameter. token Input parameter.

#### `void setEventCallback(std::function< void(const AuditEvent &)> cb)`
- Source: `include/network/network_audit_log.h`:156
- Brief: Register a sink callback invoked for every new event.
- Parameters:
  - `cb` (std::function< void(const AuditEvent &)>): Callable receiving a const-reference to the new event. Pass nullptr to clear the callback.
- Details: The callback is called outside the internal mutex, so it may safely call back into the log (e.g., to read stats). Only one callback can be registered at a time; a second call replaces the previous one. cb Callable receiving a const-reference to the new event. Pass nullptr to clear the callback.

#### `T read_big_endian(std::vector< uint8_t >::const_iterator &it, const std::vector< uint8_t >::const_iterator &end)`

- Return: Value decoded in host byte order (big-endian source assumed).
- Throws:
  - ParseError: if fewer than sizeof(T) bytes remain.
- Details: T Trivially-copyable integer type (uint8_t, uint16_t, uint32_t …). it Current buffer position (advanced by sizeof(T) on return). end One-past-the-end sentinel. Value decoded in host byte order (big-endian source assumed). ParseError if fewer than sizeof(T) bytes remain. Iterator safety: uses AdvanceSafe::advance() internally.

#### `bool allowSend(uint64_t connection_id, uint64_t bytes, std::chrono::milliseconds timeout=std::chrono::milliseconds(0))`
- Source: `include/network/qos_manager.h`:575
- Brief: Check whether bytes may be sent now (token bucket + queue check).
- Parameters:
  - `connection_id` (uint64_t): Identifier of the connection.
  - `bytes` (uint64_t): Input parameter.
  - `timeout` (std::chrono::milliseconds): Input parameter.
- Return: true if the send is permitted, false if backpressure applies.
- Details: Allow Send. If a token bucket is configured and there are insufficient tokens, the function may block up to timeout to wait for refill. If the write queue depth would exceed max_queue_bytes, it returns false immediately (backpressure signal). connection_id Target connection. bytes Number of bytes the caller wants to send. timeout Maximum time to wait for tokens. true if the send is permitted, false if backpressure applies. connection_id Identifier of the connection. bytes Input parameter. timeout Input parameter. True when the operation succeeds. Calls: findConnection(), load(), fetch_add(), cb_lock(), backpressure_cb_(), lock(), find(), end().

#### `void assignTenant(uint64_t connection_id, const std::string &tenant_id)`
- Source: `include/network/qos_manager.h`:707
- Brief: Assign a registered connection to a tenant.
- Parameters:
  - `connection_id` (uint64_t): Identifier of the connection.
  - `tenant_id` (const std::string &): Identifier of the tenant.
- Details: Assign Tenant. After assignment, allowSend will also check the tenant-level token bucket in addition to the per-connection bucket. A connection can only belong to one tenant; calling this again re-assigns it. Typically called after the client authenticates and the tenant is known. connection_id Connection to assign. tenant_id Target tenant identifier. connection_id Identifier of the connection. tenant_id Identifier of the tenant. Calls: lock(), find(), end(), empty(), findTenant(), fetch_sub(), fetch_add().

#### `bool configureTc(const TcConfig &tc_config)`
- Source: `include/network/qos_manager.h`:556
- Brief: Apply an HTB qdisc configuration on a Linux network interface.
- Parameters:
  - `tc_config` (const TcConfig &): Input parameter.
- Return: true if tc commands succeeded; false otherwise.
- Details: Configure Tc. Issues tc qdisc and tc class commands to set up a Hierarchical Token Bucket on the specified interface. On non-Linux platforms this function returns false without doing anything. Requires the process to have sufficient privileges (CAP_NET_ADMIN). tc_config tc configuration parameters. true if tc commands succeeded; false otherwise. tc_config Input parameter. True when the operation succeeds. Calls: empty(), isValidInterfaceName(), THEMIS_ERROR(), defined(), access(), c_str(), effectiveMaxBandwidthBps(), std::max().

#### `std::optional< PendingSend > dequeueForSend()`
- Source: `include/network/qos_manager.h`:487
- Brief: Dequeue the next pending send selected by priority + fair-queuing.
- Parameters: none
- Return: The next PendingSend, or std::nullopt if all queues are empty.
- Details: Dequeue For Send. Selects the highest-priority non-empty queue. Within a priority level, connections are served in round-robin order (fair queuing). A starvation guard ensures that lower-priority items are occasionally served even when higher-priority queues are always non-empty (see Config::starvation_guard_threshold). The next PendingSend, or std::nullopt if all queues are empty. Return value. Calls: lock(), empty(), front(), pop_front().

#### `bool enqueueSend(uint64_t connection_id, uint64_t bytes)`
- Source: `include/network/qos_manager.h`:473
- Brief: Enqueue a pending send into the priority scheduler.
- Parameters:
  - `connection_id` (uint64_t): Identifier of the connection.
  - `bytes` (uint64_t): Input parameter.
- Return: true if the item was accepted; false if the connection is unknown.
- Details: Enqueue Send. The send is placed into the priority queue corresponding to the connection's current priority level. The scheduler does NOT immediately consume token bucket tokens — that happens in allowSend(). connection_id Source connection. bytes Number of bytes to send. true if the item was accepted; false if the connection is unknown. connection_id Identifier of the connection. bytes Input parameter. True when the operation succeeds. Calls: findConnection(), load(), lock(), push_back().

#### `void setLeakyBucket(uint64_t connection_id, uint64_t drain_rate_bps, uint64_t capacity_bytes)`

Calls: findConnection(), lock(), reconfigure().

### themis::network::RetryPolicy

#### `std::chrono::milliseconds computeDelay(uint32_t attempt) const noexcept`
- Source: `include/network/wire_protocol_server.h`:150
- Brief: Compute the back-off delay for the given zero-indexed attempt.
- Parameters:
  - `attempt` (uint32_t): Zero-based index: 0 = delay before the 1st retry, 1 = before the 2nd, and so on.
- Return: Back-off duration capped at max_delay_ms.
- Details: Uses std::chrono::steady_clock epoch (cast to nanoseconds) as the random seed when the thread-local PRNG is first accessed so that the jitter is not deterministic across processes. The function never sleeps; callers are responsible for applying the returned duration (e.g. via std::this_thread::sleep_for). attempt Zero-based index: 0 = delay before the 1st retry, 1 = before the 2nd, and so on. Back-off duration capped at max_delay_ms.

#### `bool isTransient(int error_code) noexcept`
- Source: `include/network/wire_protocol_server.h`:159
- Brief: Return true when error_code represents a transient error.
- Parameters:
  - `error_code` (int): POSIX errno value.
- Details: Recognised codes: ECONNRESET, ETIMEDOUT, EAGAIN, EWOULDBLOCK. error_code POSIX errno value.

#### `void registerTenantQuota(const std::string &tenant_id, uint64_t rate_bps, uint64_t burst_bytes=0)`
- Source: `include/network/wire_protocol_server.h`:419
- Brief: Register or update an aggregate bandwidth quota for a tenant.
- Parameters:
  - `tenant_id` (const std::string &): Identifier of the tenant.
  - `rate_bps` (uint64_t): Input parameter.
  - `burst_bytes` (uint64_t): Input parameter.
- Details: ---------------------------------------------------------------------- Per-tenant bandwidth quota management – public forwarding API ---------------------------------------------------------------------- All connections that have been assigned to this tenant via Session::setTenant() share a single token bucket enforcing the aggregate limit. Call this during server configuration or whenever the tenant's quota changes. tenant_id Unique tenant identifier. rate_bps Sustained aggregate bandwidth in bits per second (0 = unlimited). burst_bytes Maximum burst in bytes (0 = auto: 1 s of sustained rate). tenant_id Identifier of the tenant. rate_bps Input parameter. burst_bytes Input parameter. Implements registerTenantQuota without additional internal calls.

#### `void setSpatialIndexManager(std::shared_ptr< index::SpatialIndexManager > idx)`
- Source: `include/network/wire_protocol_server.h`:465
- Brief: Inject a SpatialIndexManager for GEO_QUERY dispatch.
- Parameters:
  - `idx` (std::shared_ptr< index::SpatialIndexManager >): Input parameter.
- Details: Set Spatial Index Manager. When set, GEO_QUERY commands on this wire-protocol port are dispatched to the provided spatial index instead of returning GEO_NOT_INTEGRATED. The manager is accessed from multiple session threads and must be thread-safe. idx Shared spatial index manager; nullptr disables geo dispatch and restores the NOT_INTEGRATED fallback. idx Input parameter. Calls: std::move().

#### `void start()`
- Source: `include/network/wire_protocol_server.h`:362
- Brief: Start server in dedicated thread pool.
- Parameters: none
- Details: Start. Creates separate IO context and worker threads, isolated from HTTP server to prevent interference. Enforces transport security validation before starting. Enforces fail-closed runtime dependency checks: QueryEngine must be present and at least one geospatial backend (SpatialIndexManager or GeoQueryFn bridge) must be configured. Calls: validateTransportSecurity(), lock(), wire_bootstrap::validateRequiredBackends(), empty(), isBlankString(), hasControlCharacters(), isAuthTokenPayloadWithinLimit(), std::any_of().

#### `ssize_t writeTo(int fd) const noexcept`
- Source: `include/network/wire_protocol_zero_copy.h`:126
- Brief: Write the frame to a file descriptor using writev(2).
- Parameters:
  - `fd` (int): Open, writable file descriptor (TCP socket).
- Return: Total bytes written on success (>= 0), or -1 on error (errno is set by the underlying writev call).
- Details: The header and payload are written atomically as a scatter-gather vector; no intermediate copy is performed. fd Open, writable file descriptor (TCP socket). Total bytes written on success (>= 0), or -1 on error (errno is set by the underlying writev call).

#### `ssize_t writeToWithSendfile(int socket_fd, int payload_fd, off_t payload_offset=0, size_t sendfile_threshold=65536) const`
- Source: `include/network/wire_protocol_zero_copy.h`:159
- Brief: Large-payload path: write header via write(2), then payload via sendfile(2) / splice(2) (Linux) or sendfile(2) (macOS/FreeBSD).
- Parameters:
  - `socket_fd` (int): Destination socket file descriptor.
  - `payload_fd` (int): Source file descriptor for sendfile. Pass -1 to force fallback to writev.
  - `payload_offset` (off_t): Starting offset in the source file (bytes).
  - `sendfile_threshold` (size_t): Minimum payload size to use sendfile (bytes). Default: 65536 (64 KiB).
- Return: Total bytes written (header + payload) on success, or -1 on error (errno set by the failing syscall).
- Throws:
  - std::bad_alloc: if memory allocation fails in fallback path
- Details: For payloads ≥ sendfile_threshold bytes and where payload_fd is a valid open file descriptor, this method: Writes the 12-byte header with write(2). Transfers payload_size_ bytes from payload_fd starting at payload_offset using sendfile(2) (Linux / BSD) or splice(2) (Linux pipe path) — zero userspace copy. Falls back to writeTo() if: payload_fd < 0 (caller signals: not file-backed). payload_size_ < sendfile_threshold. sendfile(2) / splice(2) fails with EINVAL / ENOSYS (e.g., the fd is a socket, not a regular file — kernel constraint). R15: Removed noexcept guarantee to allow exception-safe buffer allocation in sendfile fallback path. Callers must handle std::bad_alloc if buffer allocation fails during fallback to pread + write. socket_fd Destination socket file descriptor. payload_fd Source file descriptor for sendfile. Pass -1 to force fallback to writev. payload_offset Starting offset in the source file (bytes). sendfile_threshold Minimum payload size to use sendfile (bytes). Default: 65536 (64 KiB). Total bytes written (header + payload) on success, or -1 on error (errno set by the failing syscall). std::bad_alloc if memory allocation fails in fallback path
